Here,\n\t// trilinear interpolation is used to calculate light level\n\t// at a given point in the node.\n\tf32 x = core::clamp(vertex_pos.X / BS + 0.5, 0.0 - SMOOTH_LIGHTING_OVERSIZE, 1.0 + loop adds infinite splits\n\t\t\t// due to e.g. precision problems.\n\t\t\t// 100 is just an arbitrary, reasonably high number.\n\t\t\tfor (size_t i = 0; i < boxes.size() && i < 100; i++) sametype_neighbors);\n\t\tdrawAutoLightedCuboid(box, tiles, 6, nullptr, mask);\n\t}\n}\n\nvoid MapblockMeshGenerator::drawMeshNode()\n{\n\tu8 facedir = 0;\n\tscene::IMesh* mesh;\n\tint degrotate = One caveat first: `/app/.harbor_assets/` is empty, so I couldn't watch `recording.mp4`. I found these bugs by reading the code against your list of expected behaviour, and I haven't checked any of them visually in the game.\n\n**Shaped blocks facing the right way** (`src/mapnode.cpp`)\n- Nodeboxes rotated by `facedir` 1 and 3 were turned the wrong way (+90° and −90° were swapped). Stairs and slabs placed facing east or west came out backwards, and their shape didn't match their textures. The mesh-block code (`rotateMeshBy6dFacedir`) already used the correct directions, so I made the two agree.\n\n**Shaped blocks drawn whole from every side** (`src/client/content_mapblock.cpp`)\n- In the list of directions used to draw nodeboxes, +X and −X were swapped. That had two effects: the east and west textures were exchanged, and a side got hidden when the block on the *opposite* side was solid. That's why faces went missing.\n\n**Blocks linking to their neighbours** (`content_mapblock.cpp`)\n- **Rails:** the angles for two curve pieces (west+north and east+south) were swapped, so the track bent the wrong way.\n- **Framed glass:** the two vertical frame edges on the east side were checking the west neighbour. Edges appeared or disappeared wrongly when panes were joined.\n\n**Selection box for wall-mounted blocks** (`mapnode.cpp`)\n- For ladders and signs on a north or south wall, the selection box was rotated to the opposite wall, so it didn't sit around the block. It now goes on the wall the block is attached to.\n\n**Overlays sinking into the block, close up or far away:** I found no fault here. Overlays are drawn at exactly the same position as the main texture. Instead of moving them, the game uses a depth-only trick (`src/client/tile.cpp`) so the overlay always wins without being drawn even slightly in front. Both kinds of nudge it needs are set (one for close views at a slant, one for distance), and I followed them through the graphics driver and the code that merges meshes. I changed nothing there, so if the video shows overlays flickering, that cause is still unfixed.\n\nThe game builds, and the unit tests pass except for `TestAddress`, a network address test unrelated to these changes. One caveat first: `/app/.harbor_assets/` is empty, so I couldn't watch `recording.mp4`. I found these bugs by reading the code against your list of expected behaviour, and I haven't checked any of them visually in the game.\n\n**Shaped blocks facing the right way** (`src/mapnode.cpp`)\n- Nodeboxes rotated by `facedir` 1 and 3 were turned the wrong way (+90° and −90° were swapped). Stairs and slabs placed facing east or west came out backwards, and their shape didn't match their textures. That had two effects: the east and west textures were exchanged, and a side got hidden when the block on the *opposite* side was solid. That's why faces went missing.\n\n**Blocks linking to their neighbours** (`content_mapblock.cpp`)\n- **Rails:** the angles for two curve pieces (west+north and east+south) were swapped, so the track bent the wrong way.\n- **Framed glass:** the two vertical frame edges on the east side were checking the west neighbour. Edges appeared or disappeared wrongly when panes were joined.\n\n**Selection box for wall-mounted blocks** (`mapnode.cpp`)\n- For ladders and signs on a north or south wall, the selection box was rotated to the opposite wall, so it didn't sit around the block. It now goes on the wall the block is attached to.\n\n**Overlays sinking into the block, close up or far away:** I found no fault here. Overlays are drawn at exactly the same position as the main texture. Instead of moving them, the game uses a depth-only trick (`src/client/tile.cpp`) so the overlay always wins without being drawn even slightly in front. Both kinds of nudge it needs are set (one for close views at a slant, one for distance), and I followed them through the graphics driver and the code that merges meshes. I changed nothing there, so if the video shows overlays flickering, that cause is still unfixed.\n\nThe game builds, and the unit tests pass except for `TestAddress`, a network address test unrelated to these changes.
